Browse All / Combined immunodeficiency due to CD3gamma deficiency

Combined immunodeficiency due to CD3gamma deficiency

Mendelian MONDO:0014276 Pathograph 64 Show in embeddings browser Combined immunodeficiency Inborn error of immunity

Autosomal recessive CD3G deficiency is a variable combined immunodeficiency caused by biallelic variants affecting the CD3gamma chain of the TCR/CD3 complex. Human patients retain variable numbers of T cells with reduced surface receptor expression, unlike the profound developmental block typical of several other CD3-chain deficiencies. Clinical presentations range from severe infantile infection and enteropathy to predominantly autoimmune disease or adult-recognized antibody deficiency. Autoimmune thyroid disease, cytopenias and other organ manifestations are frequent in published cohorts but are not universal. Altered TCR signaling, Treg number/function and repertoire selection provide a qualified immune-dysregulation model; normal Treg function and selected normal mitogen responses in some patients constrain universal claims.

Inheritance

1
Autosomal recessive inheritance HP:0000007
Biallelic CD3G variants. Almost all reported patients are homozygous, and parental consanguinity is common; two siblings from one non-consanguineous Spanish family were compound heterozygous.
Autosomal recessive inheritance
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:42661620 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"CD3γ deficiency is an ultrarare autosomal recessive inborn error of immunity characterized by immune dysregulation and variable immunodeficiency."
States the autosomal recessive mode of inheritance and the defining combination of immune dysregulation with variable immunodeficiency.
PMID:42661620 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Variants were homozygous in all but two reported cases"
Records that the biallelic variants are homozygous in nearly every reported family.

Discussions and Knowledge Gaps

9
Which developmental and receptor-composition differences explain retained human T cells, and how faithfully do these systems reproduce human tolerance defects?
HUMAN MODEL MISMATCH OPEN mismatch_cd3g_mouse_vs_human
Attached to
pathophysiology#Distorted Thymic Selection and Self-Reactive Repertoire pathophysiology#Impaired TCR/CD3 Complex Assembly and Surface Expression
Cd3g-null mice retain only 2–5% of normal peripheral T cells. Human CD3delta rescues gamma-delta development in double-deficient mice, but this does not reconstruct the human alpha-beta/Treg pathway. Mature Jurkat knockdown and congenital patient-derived cells also differ; the immature-organ-culture perturbation in the 2021 study was CD3D. None of these observations proves that autoimmunity is absent from every model or that no model can study it.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:9524111 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Peripheral lymphoid organs are also practically devoid of T cells, with absolute numbers of peripheral T cells reduced to only 2-5% of those in normal mice."
The model retains a small peripheral population; the result must not be described as complete absence.
PMID:17923503 SUPPORT Model Organism
"A human, but not a mouse, CD3delta transgene rescues gammadelta T cell development in mice lacking both mouse CD3delta and CD3gamma chains."
The perturbation demonstrates lineage-specific structural compensation in a mouse model; it does not by itself resolve human alpha-beta/Treg biology.
Which modifiers account for different clinical courses among relatives sharing a CD3G variant?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN gap_cd3g_genotype_phenotype
Attached to
genetic#CD3G pathophysiology#Multisystem Autoimmunity
Identical variants accompany divergent infection and autoimmune outcomes. Demonstrated protein loss for particular alleles shows that residual CD3gamma is not required for all retained human T-cell development; it does not exclude allele-specific effects across the whole spectrum. Preserved Taiwanese Treg function and variable age/stimulus-dependent proliferation are correlates, not validated prognostic rules.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:42661620 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Notably, patients carrying identical deleterious variants exhibited substantial variability in clinical presentation and outcomes, indicating that no obvious genotype-phenotype correlation could be established based on the currently available data."
States the absence of a genotype-phenotype correlation.
PMID:31921117 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Treg cells isolated from the CD3 γ Del 213A T cells of the patient were able to suﬃciently suppress the proliferation of Teﬀ cells upon stimulation"
The Taiwanese adult retained suppression in the reported coculture conditions, qualifying universal Treg-dysfunction claims.
How should overlapping case counts and contradictions between primary reports and later summary tables be interpreted?
INTERPRETATION OPEN interpretation_cd3g_cohorts
Attached to
phenotypes#Autoimmune thyroiditis phenotypes#Decreased circulating immunoglobulin concentration phenotypes#Impaired PHA-induced T-cell proliferation
The 2026 18-patient aggregation overlaps the 2014 families, 2018 mechanistic cohort and detailed later case reports. It is not an independent prevalence sample. Its 9/18 antibody category combines hypo- and dysgammaglobulinemia, and its 11/11 mitogen category is not PHA-specific. The detailed 2021 child had homozygous c.1A>G and died after transplantation; the later table assigns a different variant/alive status to that report. Normal Taiwanese IgG measurements were during replacement. The earlier 2007 sibling VI3 was presumed affected rather than molecularly tested. Frequencies therefore retain reported-series scope and detailed primary narratives take precedence for patient facts.

The authors give the literature count and two new cases; this provides no population denominator.

⚙

Pathophysiology

15
Biallelic CD3G Loss-of-Function Variants
Biallelic CD3G variants impair the CD3gamma subunit of the TCR/CD3 complex. Complete transcript and protein loss was demonstrated for homozygous c.1A>G in cultured blasts from one child; PBMC protein loss was also shown in the two adults reported in 2026. These results establish that human T-cell development can persist without detectable CD3gamma; they do not establish identical residual function for every reported allele. The original Spanish siblings were compound heterozygotes, not homozygotes for the initiation-codon variant.
CD3G hgnc:1675 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves CD3G (hgnc:1675). hgnc:1675 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Downstream
Impaired TCR/CD3 Complex Assembly and Surface Expression Loss of CD3gamma compromises receptor assembly and surface abundance, with residual expression varying by cellular context.
Impaired T-Cell Help for Memory B-Cell Maturation The Taiwanese report attributes a diminished CD40L response and memory-B-cell defect to CD3G deficiency, but the intervening route is unresolved.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:42661620 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Pathogenic variants included splice-site, nonsense, and frameshift mutations predicted to result in loss of function"
Describes the class of CD3G alleles reported across the literature.
PMID:33215322 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Together, these results demonstrate biallelic CD3G c.1 A > G mutation results in the complete loss of CD3G mRNA transcripts and CD3γ protein translation."
Establishes, in patient-derived T-cell blasts, that a reported allele is a true null rather than a hypomorph.
Impaired TCR/CD3 Complex Assembly and Surface Expression
CD3gamma deficiency lowers, without invariably abolishing, surface TCR/CD3 expression in human T cells. CD3G-knockdown Jurkat cells show unstable partial receptor ensembles, ER retention and deficient zeta-dimer incorporation, but their profound receptor loss does not quantitatively reproduce patient cells. Patient-cell reconstitution supports a role for CD3gamma in surface expression; the precise assembly/export constraints depend on the cellular system. Human gamma-delta receptors can incorporate CD3delta, providing a supported explanation for partial compensation in that lineage.
mature alpha-beta T cell CL:0000791 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves mature alpha-beta T cell (CL:0000791). CL:0000791 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
alpha-beta T cell receptor complex GO:0042105 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this protein complex This pathophysiological event involves decreased alpha-beta T cell receptor complex (GO:0042105). GO:0042105 is a protein complex from the Gene Ontology.
Experimental models
CD3G-knockdown human Jurkat T cells and the CD3gamma-negative JGN line CELL LINE Jurkat knockdown measures receptor assembly, ER retention and low surface export. In JGN, the CD3gamma extracellular domain is required for rescue, whereas patient-derived T-cell rescue differs. Domain requirements are therefore interpreted within each cellular system.
Animal models
Cd3g-deficient mouse Mouse partially recapitulates The mouse does reproduce the receptor-expression defect: surface TCR and CD3epsilon are severely reduced on thymocytes and peripheral T cells.
Downstream
Attenuated T-Cell Receptor Signaling Reduced and altered receptor complexes change signaling strength; signaling is attenuated selectively rather than uniformly abolished.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:42661620 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Western blot analysis of PBMC-derived protein demonstrated complete absence of CD3 g expression, while ﬂow cytometry revealed reduced expression of the TCR/CD3 complex, conﬁrming the diagnosis of CD3 g deﬁciency (Figures 1B– D)."
Patient P1 had absent protein and reduced receptor expression measured directly in PBMCs.
PMID:34249896 SUPPORT In Vitro
"However, both defective TCR ensembles were strongly retained in the ER, lacked ζζ/CD2472, and barely reached the T-cell surface (<11% of normal controls) in any of the CD3 KD cells."
The assembly/export defect was measured in engineered Jurkat knockdowns; the paper separately documents a milder defect in patient-derived cells.
Attenuated T-Cell Receptor Signaling
Reduced and structurally altered TCR/CD3 complexes change signaling in a response-dependent manner. Cultured patient-derived lines retain several proximal responses but have altered adhesion/polarization and increased activation-induced death. In reconstituted JGN cells, removal of the CD3gamma intracellular domain impaired IL-2/TNF-alpha production while preserving proximal phosphorylation and CD69 induction. These model findings do not establish global absence of T-cell activation in patients; PHA and anti-CD3/CD28 responses can be preserved in particular patients or assay conditions.

Downstream
Distorted Thymic Selection and Self-Reactive Repertoire The proposed signaling-strength model alters thymic selection and CD4-cell fate.
Regulatory T-Cell Deficiency and Impaired Suppression TCR-dependent Treg development and maintenance provide a proposed route to reduced Treg number, diversity and function.
Impaired T-Cell Proliferative Response Altered TCR signaling contributes to stimulus-dependent impairment of cell division.
Reduced Thymic Output and Altered T-Cell Homeostasis The authors' signaling-strength model alters T-cell development and homeostasis, compatible with reduced output markers despite retained peripheral T cells.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:12407027 SUPPORT In Vitro
"In contrast, other physiological responses like the specific adhesion and concomitant cell polarization on ICAM-1-coated dishes were selectively defective, and activation-induced cell death was increased."
Patient-derived mature T-cell lines had selective functional defects, despite largely preserved proximal responses.
PMID:36119034 SUPPORT In Vitro
"In contrast to cytokine synthesis, CD69 expression and TCR internalization after TCR engagement were normal for all constructs including γγ-, which scored highest for CD69 induction, indicating that it was capable of some functional signal transduction."
The full Results qualify the abstract: the intracellular-domain-truncated construct retained CD69 induction; cytokine secretion and CD69 cannot be bundled as uniformly reduced.
Distorted Thymic Selection and Self-Reactive Repertoire
Reduced TCR signaling is proposed to alter thymic selection and the fate of self-reactive CD4 cells. Peripheral repertoire sequencing in six patients with autoimmunity found reduced Treg repertoire diversity and a hydrophobic CDR3 signature associated with self-reactivity in conventional CD4 cells. This is a molecular surrogate and a mechanistic interpretation, not direct observation of failed negative selection or demonstration of each clone's autoantigen specificity. The same self-reactivity signature was not found in CD8 cells.

thymic T cell selection GO:0045061 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves decreased thymic T cell selection (GO:0045061). GO:0045061 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology. ↓ DECREASED
Animal models
Cd3g-deficient mouse Mouse fails to recapitulate The profound mouse developmental bottleneck does not reproduce the preserved, variably skewed peripheral repertoire studied in human autoimmune cohorts. The cited experiments do not directly establish fidelity for human Treg dysfunction or clinical autoimmunity.
Downstream
Multisystem Autoimmunity The inferred escape of self-reactive conventional CD4 cells can contribute to loss of tolerance; repertoire hydrophobicity is a surrogate, not direct antigen-specific proof.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:29653965 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The TRB repertoire of Tconv cells from patients with CD3G deficiency was enriched for hydrophobic amino acids at positions 6 and 7 of the CDR3, a biomarker of self-reactivity."
Peripheral repertoire sequencing found enrichment of hydrophobic CDR3 residues associated with self-reactivity; this surrogate does not directly demonstrate clone-specific autoantigen recognition or thymic negative-selection failure.
Regulatory T-Cell Deficiency and Impaired Suppression
In the 2018 autoimmune cohort, circulating Treg proportions and repertoire diversity were reduced; suppression was tested in four patients and was impaired. Reduced TCR signaling may affect Treg selection, maintenance and suppressive function, but the relative contribution of these processes remains unresolved. A Taiwanese adult had preserved Treg proportion and suppressive activity with no recognized autoimmunity. Treg deficiency is therefore an important variable mechanism, not a universal requirement for CD3G-associated disease.

Multisystem Autoimmunity Reduced Treg diversity and suppressive control can permit autoreactive-cell expansion in the affected autoimmune cohorts.

"By contrast, T reg cells from CD3G-mutated patients completely failed to suppress proliferation of control Teff cells up to a 1:2 Treg: Teff cell ratio; reduced suppressive activity was also detected at a 1:1 Treg:Tconv ratio (Figure 4B-C)."
Suppression cocultures used Tregs from four patients P2–P5; the assay does not imply that all six patients underwent functional testing.
PMID:31921117 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Treg cells isolated from the CD3 γ Del 213A T cells of the patient were able to suﬃciently suppress the proliferation of Teﬀ cells upon stimulation"
The Taiwanese adult retained suppression in the reported coculture conditions, qualifying universal Treg-dysfunction claims.
Impaired T-Cell Proliferative Response
Mitogen-driven T-cell division is impaired in several CD3G cohorts, consistent with altered receptor signaling. The response depends on stimulus, cell subset, age and assay: anti-CD3 plus CD28 restored near-control responses in the 2018 study, and the Taiwanese patient had normal PHA responses despite reduced ConA incorporation. The 2026 count of 11/11 abnormal responses to at least one mitogen does not establish universal impairment of PHA-induced transformation or predict clinical severity.

Downstream
Defective Adaptive Host Defense Deficient cellular immune responses can compromise pathogen control, while assay and patient heterogeneity preclude a deterministic severity rule.
Show evidence (2 references)
url:https://www.ucm.es/data/cont/docs/1572-2019-04-22-Blood%202018.pdf SUPPORT In Vitro
"When stimulated with phytohema gglutinin (PHA), both CD4 1 and CD81 T cells in CD3g-deﬁcient patients demonstrated reduced cellular division (as shown by CFSE dilution);"
The six-patient study measured reduced division after PHA stimulation.
url:https://www.ucm.es/data/cont/docs/1572-2019-04-22-Blood%202018.pdf SUPPORT In Vitro
"Stimulation with both anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 resulted in overall equivalent activation, proliferation, and cell division in both CD4 1 and CD81 T cells from patients and controls (Figure 2A-D)."
Costimulation preserved responses in the tested patients; this constrains a global proliferation-failure model.
Humoral Immune Failure
Humoral impairment ranges from profound low immunoglobulins and deficient vaccine responses to normal or elevated concentrations. Reduced switched-memory B cells and diminished CD40L induction in a Taiwanese patient suggest impaired T-cell help as one contributor; intrinsic B-cell dysfunction was not demonstrated by rescue or Tfh/B-cell coculture. Nephrotic protein loss, glucocorticoids and immunoglobulin replacement can also influence measured concentrations. The 2026 9/18 hypo-/dysgammaglobulinemia aggregate cannot be assigned to low total IgG alone.

Downstream
Defective Adaptive Host Defense Insufficient antibody-mediated protection contributes to bacterial respiratory infection in patients with hypogammaglobulinemia, including the Taiwanese adult with preserved PHA responses.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:33215322 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Reduced numbers of switched and unswitched memory B cells, hypogammaglobulinemia (IgG 338 mg/dL), and impaired vaccination response to Haemophilus influenzae type B and Pneumococcus were also present (Table 1)."
The child had several directly documented humoral defects before replacement therapy.
PMID:31921117 SUPPORT INDIRECT Other
"This may have been associated with insuﬃcient CD40L expression, which has been reported in some patients with CVID and hyper IgM"
The authors propose insufficient T-cell help as a contributor to the Taiwanese patient's memory-B-cell abnormality; they did not establish an intrinsic B-cell lesion.
Multisystem Autoimmunity
Defective central and peripheral tolerance can permit autoreactive T-cell activity and autoantibody-associated tissue injury. The 2018 repertoire/suppression study proposes this mechanism for the patients' autoimmunity, rather than directly demonstrating every organ-specific effector pathway. Thyroid disease, autoimmune cytopenias, enteropathy, hepatitis, vitiligo and a lupus-like presentation occur variably. The absence of autoimmunity in a patient with preserved Treg function is a scope constraint, not evidence that autoimmunity is absent from the disorder.
Downstream
Immune-Mediated Gastrointestinal Mucosal Injury Loss of immune tolerance can mediate intestinal injury in the patient with biopsy-supported autoimmune enteropathy.
Autoimmune thyroiditis Impaired tolerance permits autoimmune thyroid inflammation in selected patients. Antithyroid antibodies and variable thyroid functional states support an organ-specific autoimmune process; the precise T-cell/B-cell effectors are not resolved.
Autoimmune hemolytic anemia Loss of immune tolerance contributes to antibody-associated erythrocyte destruction in reported AIHA cases. Rituximab-responsive Evans syndrome supports a B-cell contribution, without identifying the initiating autoreactive clone.
Autoimmune thrombocytopenia Tolerance failure permits immune-mediated platelet loss in reported ITP/Evans syndrome. The receptor/Treg model is an upstream explanation; the relative platelet-clearance and production mechanisms were not established.
Autoimmune hepatitis The tolerance-defect model provides a qualified contribution to the explicitly diagnosed autoimmune hepatitis in the Turkish series. This is separate from the unconfirmed hepatic diagnosis in the Taiwanese adult.
Vitiligo Defective immune tolerance offers a proposed contribution to the reported autoimmune depigmentation. The CD3G studies do not directly measure melanocyte-specific effector cells or explain why only some patients develop vitiligo.
Minimal change glomerulonephritis The primary authors classify the single minimal-change nephrotic presentation among autoimmune manifestations. The tolerance model is a qualified upstream contribution; no particular podocyte-targeting antibody or T-cell mediator was established.
Lupus-like systemic autoimmunity The 2024 authors attribute a lupus-like syndrome meeting SLICC criteria to CD3G deficiency. The tolerance model provides an indirect explanation, while organ-specific immune mechanisms remain unresolved.
Hypothyroidism Autoimmune thyroid disease can produce hypothyroidism in the reported patients; euthyroid thyroiditis and the opposite functional state also occur.
Hyperthyroidism Autoimmune thyroid disease can produce hyperthyroidism in the reported patients; euthyroid thyroiditis and the opposite functional state also occur.
Interstitial lung disease Immune dysregulation contributed to the 2021 child's interstitial lung disease as suggested by partial improvement during immunosuppression. EBER-positive infiltrates and bacterial infection make this a mixed, incompletely resolved attribution.

url:https://www.ucm.es/data/cont/docs/1572-2019-04-22-Blood%202018.pdf SUPPORT INDIRECT Other
"Ultimately, this results in enrichment for self-reactive speciﬁ cities within CD4 1 Tconv cells, which may undergo peripheral activation and expansion because of the reduced number and impaired function of T reg cells. Alto- gether, these abnormalities create an environment that promotes the..."
The authors' integrated model links altered selection and Treg control to autoimmunity; organ-specific effectors and penetrance remain incompletely resolved.
Defective Adaptive Host Defense
Impaired T-cell effector responses and, in some patients, antibody deficiency reduce adaptive control of infection. Clinical expression ranges from recurrent sinopulmonary bacterial infections to severe viral, fungal and intestinal infections. Preserved PHA responses and Treg function in the Taiwanese adult coexist with humoral failure and recurrent respiratory infection. Neither a single proliferation assay nor surface receptor intensity is a validated predictor of infection severity.
Downstream
Chronic Infection-Associated Airway Injury Repeated respiratory infection contributes to structural airway injury in reported patients.
Recurrent respiratory infections Deficient adaptive pathogen control contributes to recurrent respiratory infections in the reported infection-prone patients; exposure, treatment and residual function modify the outcome.
Recurrent oral thrush Deficient adaptive pathogen control contributes to recurrent oral thrush in the reported infection-prone patients; exposure, treatment and residual function modify the outcome.
Severe Epstein-Barr virus infection Deficient adaptive pathogen control contributes to severe epstein-barr virus infection in the reported infection-prone patients; exposure, treatment and residual function modify the outcome.
Mastoiditis Recurrent sinopulmonary infection led to mastoiditis in the Taiwanese adult, representing a local infectious complication.
Pleural effusion Recurrent sinopulmonary infection led to pleural effusion in the Taiwanese adult, representing a local infectious complication.
Severe varicella infection Impaired cellular host defense provides a qualified contribution to severe varicella in the reported patients; viral exposure and residual function are not resolved by the clinical table.

"Patients with opportunistic infections, including parainﬂuenza pneumonia, Candida albicans , Giardia intestinalis, and severe EBV infection, reﬂect profoundly impaired lymphocyte proliferation"
The authors relate opportunistic infection to impaired cellular immunity, based on reported cases rather than a prospective causal experiment.
PMID:39094808 SUPPORT INDIRECT Other
"CD3G gene encodes one of the CD3 subunits named CD3γ, and its deficiency can cause autoimmune disorders, immunodeficiency and recurrent infections."
The clinical report summarizes the causal association between CD3G deficiency and impaired host defense.
Chronic Infection-Associated Airway Injury
Repeated sinopulmonary infection can produce structural airway damage and bronchiectasis. The Taiwanese case explicitly describes bronchiectasis as a complication of recurrent infections and later links worsening bronchiectasis to increased pulmonary artery pressure and cor pulmonale. The route is supported within that longitudinal case and is not a claim that every patient develops permanent lung disease.
Downstream
Bronchiectasis Cumulative infection-associated airway injury produces permanent airway dilatation in the reported respiratory disease cases.
Finger clubbing The Taiwanese authors attribute finger clubbing to chronic endobronchial disease. This case-level causal interpretation is not extrapolated to all patients.
Obstructive sleep apnea The Taiwanese authors attribute obstructive sleep apnea to chronic endobronchial disease. This case-level causal interpretation is not extrapolated to all patients.
Exertional tachypnea The Taiwanese authors attribute exertional tachypnea to chronic endobronchial disease. This case-level causal interpretation is not extrapolated to all patients.
Pulmonary hypertension Worsening bronchiectasis was reported to raise pulmonary artery pressure and progress to cor pulmonale in the Taiwanese patient; this is a case-specific sequence.
Cor pulmonale Worsening bronchiectasis was reported to raise pulmonary artery pressure and progress to cor pulmonale in the Taiwanese patient; this is a case-specific sequence.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:31921117 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"He experienced recurrent sinopulmonary infections that were complicated by mastoiditis, pleural eﬀusion, and bronchiecta sis (Supplemental Figure 1A) and required hospitalization from the age of 14-years."
The longitudinal case attributes these pulmonary/ENT complications to recurrent infection.
Impaired T-Cell Help for Memory B-Cell Maturation
In the Taiwanese adult, stimulated CD4 cells expressed about half the control CD40L level and class-switched memory B cells were reduced. The authors propose insufficient CD40L-dependent help as a contributor to B-cell maturation failure. CD40L was measured after PMA/ionomycin, not direct TCR triggering; the CD3G-to-CD40L route and its prevalence remain unresolved, and the proposed Tfh/B-cell coculture was not performed.
Downstream
Humoral Immune Failure Reduced CD40L-dependent T-cell help is a proposed contributor to deficient memory-B-cell maturation and humoral responses in the Taiwanese patient.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:31921117 SUPPORT In Vitro
"The CD40L expression of activated CD4 + in the patient was around half that of the healthy control, while CD69 expression was used as an a ctivation marker after stimulation with 10 ng/mL PMA and 1 ug/m L ionomycin for 5 h. T wo duplicates were performed."
Stimulated patient-cell measurement; it does not directly test TCR-dependent induction or rescue of B-cell maturation.
Immune-Mediated Gastrointestinal Mucosal Injury
Autoimmune enteropathy with anti-enterocyte reactivity was demonstrated in the 2021 child, together with chronic active gastritis and colitis. Persistent gastrointestinal mucosal injury can account for diarrhea and nutritional failure; improvement during immunosuppression and in another patient after HSCT supports an immune contribution. The gastric contribution is inferred from the overall inflammatory presentation rather than a demonstrated stomach-specific effector. This mechanism is not assigned to every infectious or unclassified episode of diarrhea.
Downstream
Protracted diarrhea Biopsy-supported autoimmune enteropathy provides a local cause of persistent diarrhea in the 2021 child, with improvement during combined immunosuppression; infection-associated diarrhea in other cases is not assigned this route.
Failure to thrive The 2021 clinical course explicitly links worsening gastrointestinal disease to growth failure and parenteral nutrition; improvement of diarrhea and weight during immunosuppression supports this case-scoped sequence.
Chronic colitis Anti-enterocyte reactivity and autoimmune enteropathy provide an immune explanation for the associated chronic active colitis in the 2021 child. They do not identify a single pathogenic autoantibody as the proven effector.
Anoperineal fistula The transplant report attributes fistula improvement to improving inflammatory bowel disease, supporting a qualified local inflammatory contribution in that infant.
Chronic gastritis The biopsy-supported autoimmune gastrointestinal presentation provides a qualified immune contribution to accompanying chronic active gastritis; a stomach-specific effector was not isolated.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:33215322 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Upper and lower endoscopy showed chronic active gastritis and colitis, and biopsies of small intestine and colon were consistent with autoimmune enteropathy. Linear staining along the periapical border of the enterocytes was positive for anti-enterocyte auto-antibodies."
Clinical pathology supports immune-mediated intestinal injury in this child; antibody positivity alone does not establish the entire effector pathway.
Suspected Nodular Regenerative Hepatic Remodeling
The Taiwanese adult had imaging and clinical findings interpreted as nodular regenerative hyperplasia. Liver biopsy was declined. The authors favored a slowly progressive portal-hypertensive pattern after an unsuccessful steroid trial for possible autoimmune hepatitis. The upstream relation to CD3G/humoral deficiency is not established; the local hepatic-to-portal/splenic interpretation remains provisional.
Downstream
Portal hypertension The authors' suspected NRH pattern provides a qualified local explanation for portal hypertension. Unconfirmed histology and unresolved upstream genetic attribution limit this route.
Splenomegaly The authors' suspected NRH pattern provides a qualified local explanation for splenomegaly. Unconfirmed histology and unresolved upstream genetic attribution limit this route.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:31921117 SUPPORT INDIRECT Other
"V ascular engorgement of the portal vein, uneven liver surfac e, tortuous splenic artery, splenomegaly, and poor response to steroids were characteristic of NRH classiﬁcation II in our patient with the CD3 γ Del 213A mutation"
The authors interpret a constellation of clinical/imaging findings as a portal-hypertensive NRH pattern, without histological confirmation.
PMID:31921117 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"he was unwilling to undergo a liver biopsy for pathological proof."
The proposed hepatic substrate was not biopsy-confirmed.
Reduced Thymic Output and Altered T-Cell Homeostasis
Human CD3G case studies infer reduced thymic output from low TRECs and naive-cell numbers despite preservation or later accumulation of peripheral polyclonal memory T cells. Absolute CD4/CD8 counts vary, and TCR/CD3-dependent gating can underestimate cells. The relative effects of production, selection, peripheral survival and treatment cannot be separated in every patient.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:17277165 SUPPORT INDIRECT Other
"Thus, the peripheral T lymphocyte pool was comparatively well preserved in human CD3gamma deficiencies despite poor thymus output or clinical outcome."
The human series distinguishes reduced output from a complete developmental block.
Helicobacter pylori-Associated Gastric Disease
Adult P2 developed H. pylori-associated gastric MALT lymphoma, which entered remission after eradication. This treatment response supports a local infectious contribution to the lymphoma; the report does not establish that CD3G deficiency caused the infection or conferred a general lymphoma predisposition.
Downstream
Gastric MALT lymphoma The reported H. pylori association and complete remission following eradication support a case-scoped infectious contribution to gastric MALT lymphoma. The intervening cellular mechanism and upstream CD3G attribution were not demonstrated.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:42661620 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"At 22 years of age, she was diagnosed with Helicobacter pylori -associated gastric mucosa-associated lymphoid tissue (MALT) lymphoma. Eradication therapy resulted in complete remission."
The lymphoma and eradication response are patient observations; HPO gastric lymphoma is broader than the source's MALT subtype.

Glucocorticoids and other immunosuppression for autoimmune manifestations
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986

Platform: Small molecule
Steroids and other immunosuppressants were used for autoimmune cytopenias and enteropathy. A child improved on combined rituximab, sirolimus and steroids, with partial lung improvement; the individual drug contributions cannot be isolated. In the adult report, immunological deterioration during glucocorticoid exposure and later tapering after rituximab support caution. The authors propose additional suppression of already impaired TCR signaling, rather than demonstrating that mechanism experimentally in these patients.
Mechanism Target:
INHIBITS Multisystem Autoimmunity — Combined immunosuppression improved the child's diarrhea and AIHA and partially improved lung disease. This observed response supports suppression of an immune-mediated component but cannot separate steroid, sirolimus and rituximab effects.

Clinical response supports an immune contribution but was incomplete and occurred with infection-directed treatment.
MODULATES Attenuated T-Cell Receptor Signaling — The authors propose that glucocorticoid inhibition of TCR signaling could worsen host defense in CD3G deficiency. MODULATES records this proposed downward signaling effect; it is not a directly measured disease-specific pharmacodynamic result.

Hematopoietic stem cell transplantation
Action: hematopoietic cell transplantationNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is hematopoietic cell transplantation (NCIT:C15431). NCIT:C15431 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Hematopoietic Cell Transplantation NCIT:C15431
Platform: Cell therapy
Allogeneic HSCT has been used in severe infantile disease. One infant showed donor chimerism, increased surface TCR expression and improved intestinal disease before dying on day 50 from infection. Other reports also describe transplant-associated deaths. These small, selected cases support possible immune restoration but do not establish a comparative survival benefit or a universal indication.
Mechanism Target:
RESTORES Impaired TCR/CD3 Complex Assembly and Surface Expression — Donor-derived T cells carry an intact CD3G gene, restoring surface TCR/CD3 expression; in one transplanted infant TCR alpha/beta expression rose to 66% with full donor chimerism.
Show evidence (1 reference)

"On day +19 after second transplantation, the CD3 TCR alpha/beta chain expression increased to 66% with development of full donor chimerism (98.6%)."
Direct measurement of restored surface receptor expression after engraftment.

Biochemical Markers

2
Serum IgG (Decreased)
Context: Low IgG is variable: the detailed child had IgG 338 mg/dL and impaired vaccine responses, and the Taiwanese adult had profound deficiency before replacement. The 9/18 aggregate combines hypo- and dysgammaglobulinemia and is not an IgG-specific frequency.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:33215322 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Reduced numbers of switched and unswitched memory B cells, hypogammaglobulinemia (IgG 338 mg/dL), and impaired vaccination response to Haemophilus influenzae type B and Pneumococcus were also present (Table 1)."
The child had several directly documented humoral defects before replacement therapy.
CD4+ T-lymphocyte count (Decreased)
Context: Absolute CD4 counts can be low despite preserved total T-cell counts; other patients have normal counts. The 2021 report calls 627 cells/μl lymphopenic, whereas the earlier cohort table used a different reference interval. Age, laboratory ranges and receptor-dependent gating require attention.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:33215322 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Immunological evaluations revealed a combined immunodeficiency with normal absolute CD3+ T cell numbers, CD4+ T cell lymphopenia (627 cells/μl) with a reduced proportion of naïve T cells and T regulatory cells, increased frequency of memory CD4+, CD8+ cells, and CD45RA+CCR7− CD8+ T cells (TEMRA)."
This report labels the child's measured CD4 count as low; it does not establish universal lymphopenia or universal normal total T-cell numbers.

Diagnosis

2
Lymphocyte immunophenotyping with surface TCR/CD3 quantitation
Flow cytometry assesses surface TCR/CD3 abundance alongside absolute total and subset T-cell counts. Reduced receptor expression can be informative even with preserved counts, but normal counts and impaired PHA responses are not obligatory findings.
flow cytometry NCIT:C16585 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Results: Reduced surface receptor expression with variable absolute T-cell and subset counts; naive and regulatory proportions may be reduced.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:42661620 SUPPORT Other
"that assessment of abTCR and CD3 z surface expression may represent a useful diagnostic marker in patients with suspected CD3g"
The authors propose surface expression as a diagnostic marker; this is interpretive guidance, not a validated sensitivity/specificity estimate.
CD3G sequencing
Molecular confirmation by gene panel, exome or genome sequencing. Because the phenotype ranges from a CVID-like adult presentation to infantile SCID-like disease, the gene is reached by different panels in different patients.
genetic testing NCIT:C15709 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Results: Biallelic loss-of-function CD3G variants.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:42661620 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Whole-genome sequencing was used to identify pathogenic CD3G variants, and protein expression was assessed by Western blot analysis."
Documents the sequencing route to diagnosis.

Prevalence

1
Worldwide
Cases In Literature Ultra Rare
The 2026 paper aggregates 16 previously reported patients and two new adults. This is a published-case count, not population prevalence or an independent cohort. Its assertion of universal genetic confirmation is limited by the earlier report of one clinically presumed, untested affected sibling, and several table entries conflict with the detailed primary reports.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:42661620 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"To date, only 16 predominantly pediatric cases have been reported. Here, we describe two additional adult patients with CD3γ deficiency and provide a comprehensive reevaluation of all previously published cases."
The authors give the literature count and two new cases; this provides no population denominator.
🔀

Differential Diagnoses

2

Conditions with similar clinical presentations that must be differentiated from Combined immunodeficiency due to CD3gamma deficiency:

CD3delta, CD3epsilon and CD3zeta deficiencies MONDO:0015974
Overlapping Features CD3D, CD3E and CD247/CD3zeta deficiencies typically present with classical SCID and profound T-cell developmental impairment. CD3G deficiency more often retains peripheral T cells, but severe clinical SCID-like CD3G presentations also occur; gene and allele-specific evaluation is required.
Distinguishing Features
  • Typical profound T-cell depletion in other CD3-chain defects
  • Variable retained T-cell numbers and autoimmunity in CD3G deficiency
  • Gene-specific molecular diagnosis rather than clinical severity alone
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:40147628 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Our results show that patients with CD3δ, CD3ε, and CD3ζ deficiencies typically present with a classic SCID phenotype. In contrast, patients with CD3γ deficiency may either show an SCID phenotype or a milder, less severe CID phenotype."
The comparative CD3-subunit analysis supports a typical rather than absolute distinction; its mixed 49-patient denominator is not assigned to CD3G alone.
Common variable immunodeficiency MONDO:0015517
Overlapping Features Adult patients with CD3G deficiency have been managed as CVID because of antibody deficiency and low memory B cells. Reduced surface TCR/CD3 and biallelic CD3G variants identify the cellular lesion; a normal PHA response, as in the Taiwanese patient, does not exclude it.
Distinguishing Features
  • Reduced surface TCRalphabeta and CD3 expression
  • Variable proliferative defects; normal PHA does not exclude CD3G deficiency
  • Biallelic CD3G variants on sequencing
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:31921117 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"This patient had the common variable immunodeficiency (CVID) phenotype and received regular immunoglobulin infusions over 20-years; he gradually developed nodular regenerative hyperplasia over a 5-year period."
A CD3gamma-deficient adult managed for two decades as common variable immunodeficiency.

Experimental Models

1
CD3G-knockdown human Jurkat T cells and the CD3gamma-negative JGN line CELL_LINE
Human T-cell lines in which CD3gamma is absent or knocked down: the JGN variant of Jurkat, which lacks CD3gamma and has no surface TCR, and stable short-hairpin knockdowns of CD3G in Jurkat E6-1. They are the systems in which the assembly and signalling roles of individual CD3gamma domains have been dissected, using domain-swap chimeras with CD3delta.
Organism
human NCBITaxon:9606 NCBI Taxonomy (NCBITaxon) Relation: this experimental model is built in this organism This experimental model is built in human, annotated with Homo sapiens (NCBITaxon:9606). NCBITaxon:9606 is an organism from the NCBI Taxonomy.
Cell source
Immortalized human T-cell leukaemia lines (Jurkat E6-1, JGN)
Culture
Two-dimensional suspension culture with lentiviral shRNA knockdown or retroviral domain-swap reconstitution
Publication
PMID:34249896
Pathograph links
Impaired TCR/CD3 Complex Assembly and Surface Expression partially recapitulates fidelity: moderate Jurkat knockdown measures receptor assembly, ER retention and low surface export. In JGN, the CD3gamma extracellular domain is required for rescue, whereas patient-derived T-cell rescue differs. Domain requirements are therefore interpreted within each cellular system.
Limitations: Knockdown lines have less than 11% surface expression, compared with greater than 30% in congenital patient-derived cells. Developmental adaptation is proposed, but the immature thymic-organ-culture perturbation used shCD3D, not shCD3G. JGN may carry additional mutations; patient-cell limitations restricted biochemical/nanocluster analyses to JGN.
Readouts
Surface TCR expression in CD3G-knockdown Jurkat cells
Direction: DECREASED
Interpretation: Confirms that CD3gamma loss impairs receptor export in a purely human system, while showing the magnitude is model-dependent.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34249896 SUPPORT In Vitro
"However, both defective TCR ensembles were strongly retained in the ER, lacked ζζ/CD2472, and barely reached the T-cell surface (<11% of normal controls) in any of the CD3 KD cells."
The measurement of residual surface receptor in the knockdown lines.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34249896 SUPPORT In Vitro
"However, both defective TCR ensembles were strongly retained in the ER, lacked ζζ/CD2472, and barely reached the T-cell surface (<11% of normal controls) in any of the CD3 KD cells."
The assembly/export defect was measured in engineered Jurkat knockdowns; the paper separately documents a milder defect in patient-derived cells.
Attenuated T-Cell Receptor Signaling measures fidelity: moderate Reconstituting the CD3gamma-negative JGN line with chimeric CD3gamma/CD3delta constructs measures which signalling outputs require which CD3gamma domain.
Limitations: JGN is a mutagenized leukemia-derived line with possible additional defects. Cytokine/nanocluster experiments were limited to JGN; proximal signaling and CD69 can remain intact despite impaired cytokine production, and these results are not clinical cytokine measurements.
Readouts
TCR-induced IL-2 and TNF-alpha production
Direction: DECREASED
Interpretation: The intracellular-domain-truncated construct had reduced cytokine production, while CD69 induction was normal/highest. The two response classes are not a single uniformly reduced activation readout.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:36119034 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Quantification of IL-2 and TNF-α showed differences among the constructs, with γγ- inducing a very poor response compared to γγγ, γγδ or γδδ."
The Results specifically support reduced cytokine output by the truncated construct.
+ 1 more reference
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36119034 SUPPORT In Vitro
"In contrast to cytokine synthesis, CD69 expression and TCR internalization after TCR engagement were normal for all constructs including γγ-, which scored highest for CD69 induction, indicating that it was capable of some functional signal transduction."
The full Results qualify the abstract: the intracellular-domain-truncated construct retained CD69 induction; cytokine secretion and CD69 cannot be bundled as uniformly reduced.
🐁

Animal Models

1
Cd3g-deficient mouse
Cd3g-null mice have thymic cellularity below 1% and peripheral T-cell numbers at 2–5% of normal, with severe impairment of both TCR lineages. This differs from the substantial peripheral T-cell compartment retained by many human patients, although human clinical disease can also be severe. Residual mouse cells are not described as wholly absent.
Species
Mouse
Genotype
Cd3g null (selective loss of CD3gamma expression)
Publication
PMID:9524111
Pathograph links
Impaired TCR/CD3 Complex Assembly and Surface Expression partially recapitulates fidelity: moderate The mouse does reproduce the receptor-expression defect: surface TCR and CD3epsilon are severely reduced on thymocytes and peripheral T cells.
Limitations: The developmental and quantitative receptor deficits are more severe than in typical human cases. This limits direct translation to mature patient T cells, without proving that no mature-cell question can be studied in the model.
Readouts
Surface TCR and CD3epsilon expression on thymocytes and peripheral T cells
Direction: DECREASED
Interpretation: The receptor-expression component of the human lesion is present in the mouse.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:9524111 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Furthermore, absence of CD3gamma results in a severe reduction in the level of TCR and CD3epsilon expression at the cell surface of thymocytes and peripheral T cells."
Direct measurement of the surface receptor deficit in the model.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:9524111 SUPPORT Model Organism
"We generated mice selectively lacking expression of CD3gamma, in which expression of CD3delta, CD3epsilon, CD3zeta, pTalpha and TCRbeta remained undisturbed."
Establishes that the model isolates loss of CD3gamma, the same lesion as in patients.
Distorted Thymic Selection and Self-Reactive Repertoire fails to recapitulate fidelity: low The profound mouse developmental bottleneck does not reproduce the preserved, variably skewed peripheral repertoire studied in human autoimmune cohorts. The cited experiments do not directly establish fidelity for human Treg dysfunction or clinical autoimmunity.
Limitations: Peripheral mouse T cells are reduced to 2–5%, not absent. Human CD3delta rescues gamma-delta development in double-deficient mice, explaining a lineage-specific compensation mechanism; this does not fully explain preserved human alpha-beta development or establish all autoimmune outcomes in the model.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:9524111 SUPPORT Model Organism
"The number of cells in the thymus is reduced to <1% of that in normal mice, and the large majority of thymocytes lack CD4 and CD8 and are arrested at the CD44-CD25+ double negative (DN) stage of development."
The mouse developmental bottleneck is much more profound than that in human cohorts retaining peripheral T cells.
+ 2 more references
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:9524111 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Peripheral lymphoid organs are also practically devoid of T cells, with absolute numbers of peripheral T cells reduced to only 2-5% of those in normal mice."
The model retains a small peripheral population; the result must not be described as complete absence.
PMID:17923503 SUPPORT Model Organism
"A human, but not a mouse, CD3delta transgene rescues gammadelta T cell development in mice lacking both mouse CD3delta and CD3gamma chains."
The perturbation demonstrates lineage-specific structural compensation in a mouse model; it does not by itself resolve human alpha-beta/Treg biology.

name: Combined immunodeficiency due to CD3gamma deficiency
creation_date: "2026-08-28T00:00:00Z"
category: Mendelian
synonyms:
- CD3gamma deficiency
- CD3-gamma deficiency
- CD3 deficiency
- immunodeficiency 17
- immunodeficiency type 17
- IMD17
- immunodeficiency 17, CD3 gamma deficient
- SCID-like immunodeficiency, T cell-partial, B cell-positive, NK cell-positive
description: >-
  Autosomal recessive CD3G deficiency is a variable combined immunodeficiency caused by biallelic variants
  affecting the CD3gamma chain of the TCR/CD3 complex. Human patients retain variable numbers of T cells
  with reduced surface receptor expression, unlike the profound developmental block typical of several
  other CD3-chain deficiencies. Clinical presentations range from severe infantile infection and enteropathy
  to predominantly autoimmune disease or adult-recognized antibody deficiency. Autoimmune thyroid disease,
  cytopenias and other organ manifestations are frequent in published cohorts but are not universal. Altered
  TCR signaling, Treg number/function and repertoire selection provide a qualified immune-dysregulation
  model; normal Treg function and selected normal mitogen responses in some patients constrain universal
  claims.

  iuis_category:
    classification_value: combined immunodeficiency
    notes: >-
      IUIS 2022 places CD3G deficiency among defects affecting cellular and humoral immunity. Its normal-T-cell-number/low-TCR-expression
      row describes a characteristic pattern, not an invariant diagnostic requirement; clinical reports
      include absolute subset lymphopenia and severe SCID-like presentations.

  description: >-
    Biallelic CD3G variants. Almost all reported patients are homozygous, and

    Spanish family were compound heterozygous.

  notes: >-
    The 2026 paper aggregates 16 previously reported patients and two new adults. This is a published-case
    count, not population prevalence or an independent cohort. Its assertion of universal genetic confirmation
    is limited by the earlier report of one clinically presumed, untested affected sibling, and several
    table entries conflict with the detailed primary reports.

pathophysiology:
- name: Biallelic CD3G Loss-of-Function Variants
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: >-
    Biallelic CD3G variants impair the CD3gamma subunit of the TCR/CD3 complex. Complete transcript and
    protein loss was demonstrated for homozygous c.1A>G in cultured blasts from one child; PBMC protein
    loss was also shown in the two adults reported in 2026. These results establish that human T-cell
    development can persist without detectable CD3gamma; they do not establish identical residual function
    for every reported allele. The original Spanish siblings were compound heterozygotes, not homozygotes
    for the initiation-codon variant.

- name: Impaired TCR/CD3 Complex Assembly and Surface Expression
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: >-
    CD3gamma deficiency lowers, without invariably abolishing, surface TCR/CD3 expression in human T cells.
    CD3G-knockdown Jurkat cells show unstable partial receptor ensembles, ER retention and deficient zeta-dimer
    incorporation, but their profound receptor loss does not quantitatively reproduce patient cells. Patient-cell
    reconstitution supports a role for CD3gamma in surface expression; the precise assembly/export constraints
    depend on the cellular system. Human gamma-delta receptors can incorporate CD3delta, providing a supported
    explanation for partial compensation in that lineage.

- name: Attenuated T-Cell Receptor Signaling
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: >-
    Reduced and structurally altered TCR/CD3 complexes change signaling in a response-dependent manner.
    Cultured patient-derived lines retain several proximal responses but have altered adhesion/polarization
    and increased activation-induced death. In reconstituted JGN cells, removal of the CD3gamma intracellular
    domain impaired IL-2/TNF-alpha production while preserving proximal phosphorylation and CD69 induction.
    These model findings do not establish global absence of T-cell activation in patients; PHA and anti-CD3/CD28
    responses can be preserved in particular patients or assay conditions.

- name: Distorted Thymic Selection and Self-Reactive Repertoire
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: >-
    Reduced TCR signaling is proposed to alter thymic selection and the fate of self-reactive CD4 cells.
    Peripheral repertoire sequencing in six patients with autoimmunity found reduced Treg repertoire diversity
    and a hydrophobic CDR3 signature associated with self-reactivity in conventional CD4 cells. This is
    a molecular surrogate and a mechanistic interpretation, not direct observation of failed negative
    selection or demonstration of each clone's autoantigen specificity. The same self-reactivity signature
    was not found in CD8 cells.

    explanation: >-
      Peripheral repertoire sequencing found enrichment of hydrophobic CDR3 residues associated with self-reactivity;

- name: Regulatory T-Cell Deficiency and Impaired Suppression
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: >-
    In the 2018 autoimmune cohort, circulating Treg proportions and repertoire diversity were reduced;
    suppression was tested in four patients and was impaired. Reduced TCR signaling may affect Treg selection,
    maintenance and suppressive function, but the relative contribution of these processes remains unresolved.
    A Taiwanese adult had preserved Treg proportion and suppressive activity with no recognized autoimmunity.
    Treg deficiency is therefore an important variable mechanism, not a universal requirement for CD3G-associated
    disease.

- name: Impaired T-Cell Proliferative Response
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: >-
    Mitogen-driven T-cell division is impaired in several CD3G cohorts, consistent with altered receptor
    signaling. The response depends on stimulus, cell subset, age and assay: anti-CD3 plus CD28 restored
    near-control responses in the 2018 study, and the Taiwanese patient had normal PHA responses despite
    reduced ConA incorporation. The 2026 count of 11/11 abnormal responses to at least one mitogen does
    not establish universal impairment of PHA-induced transformation or predict clinical severity.

- name: Humoral Immune Failure
  biological_scale: ORGANISM
  description: >-
    Humoral impairment ranges from profound low immunoglobulins and deficient vaccine responses to normal
    or elevated concentrations. Reduced switched-memory B cells and diminished CD40L induction in a Taiwanese
    patient suggest impaired T-cell help as one contributor; intrinsic B-cell dysfunction was not demonstrated
    by rescue or Tfh/B-cell coculture. Nephrotic protein loss, glucocorticoids and immunoglobulin replacement
    can also influence measured concentrations. The 2026 9/18 hypo-/dysgammaglobulinemia aggregate cannot
    be assigned to low total IgG alone.

- name: Multisystem Autoimmunity
  biological_scale: ORGANISM
  description: >-
    Defective central and peripheral tolerance can permit autoreactive T-cell activity and autoantibody-associated
    tissue injury. The 2018 repertoire/suppression study proposes this mechanism for the patients' autoimmunity,
    rather than directly demonstrating every organ-specific effector pathway. Thyroid disease, autoimmune
    cytopenias, enteropathy, hepatitis, vitiligo and a lupus-like presentation occur variably. The absence
    of autoimmunity in a patient with preserved Treg function is a scope constraint, not evidence that
    autoimmunity is absent from the disorder.
  evidence:
  - reference: url:https://www.ucm.es/data/cont/docs/1572-2019-04-22-Blood%202018.pdf
    reference_title: https://www.ucm.es/data/cont/docs/1572-2019-04-22-Blood%202018.pdf
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: |-
      Ultimately, this results in
      enrichment for self-reactive speciﬁ cities within CD4
      1 Tconv cells,
      which may undergo peripheral activation and expansion because
      of the reduced number and impaired function of T
      reg cells. Alto-
      gether, these abnormalities create an environment that promotes
      the development of autoimmunity in this condition.
    explanation: >-
      The authors' integrated model links altered selection and Treg control to autoimmunity; organ-specific
      effectors and penetrance remain incompletely resolved.
    directness: INDIRECT

    case explicitly describes bronchiectasis as a complication of recurrent infections and later links

- name: Reduced Thymic Output and Altered T-Cell Homeostasis
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: >-
    Human CD3G case studies infer reduced thymic output from low TRECs and naive-cell numbers despite
    preservation or later accumulation of peripheral polyclonal memory T cells. Absolute CD4/CD8 counts

genetic:
- name: CD3G
  association: Causal biallelic variant
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: CD3G
    term:
      id: hgnc:1675
      label: CD3G
  notes: >-
    Reported variants include c.80-1G>C, initiation-codon and truncating changes. The original Spanish
    siblings were compound heterozygotes involving c.1A>G and a splice-site variant; a different child
    was homozygous for c.1A>G. Identical homozygous variants can produce markedly different courses, and
    protein-null status has not been measured for every allele.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:42661620
    reference_title: "Expanding the clinical spectrum of CD3γ deficiency: comprehensive characterization
      of adult-onset disease and integrated reevaluation of all reported patients."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Notably, patients carrying identical deleterious variants exhibited substantial variability in clinical
      presentation and outcomes, indicating that no obvious genotype-phenotype correlation could be established
      based on the currently available data.
    explanation: >-
      States the absence of a genotype-phenotype correlation.

      immunodeficiency and their variable clinical and cellular phenotypes despite the same homozygous

- name: Glucocorticoids and other immunosuppression for autoimmune manifestations
  description: >-
    Steroids and other immunosuppressants were used for autoimmune cytopenias and enteropathy. A child
    improved on combined rituximab, sirolimus and steroids, with partial lung improvement; the individual
    drug contributions cannot be isolated. In the adult report, immunological deterioration during glucocorticoid
    exposure and later tapering after rituximab support caution. The authors propose additional suppression
    of already impaired TCR signaling, rather than demonstrating that mechanism experimentally in these
    patients.

- name: Hematopoietic stem cell transplantation
  description: >-
    Allogeneic HSCT has been used in severe infantile disease. One infant showed donor chimerism, increased
    surface TCR expression and improved intestinal disease before dying on day 50 from infection. Other
    reports also describe transplant-associated deaths. These small, selected cases support possible immune
    restoration but do not establish a comparative survival benefit or a universal indication.
  therapeutic_modality: CELL_THERAPY

        inflammatory bowel disease.

      explanation: >-

      Three patients died, one from a severe infection at 31 months, one from post-transplant respiratory
      failure due to viral pneumonia at 17 months, and one from graft-vs.-host disease at 47 months.
    explanation: >-
      Records the transplant-related deaths behind the statement that outcomes
      have been poor.
diagnosis:
- name: Lymphocyte immunophenotyping with surface TCR/CD3 quantitation
  description: >-
    Flow cytometry assesses surface TCR/CD3 abundance alongside absolute total and subset T-cell counts.
    Reduced receptor expression can be informative even with preserved counts, but normal counts and impaired
    PHA responses are not obligatory findings.
  results: >-
    Reduced surface receptor expression with variable absolute T-cell and subset counts; naive and regulatory
    proportions may be reduced.

differential_diagnoses:
- name: CD3delta, CD3epsilon and CD3zeta deficiencies
  description: >-
    CD3D, CD3E and CD247/CD3zeta deficiencies typically present with classical SCID and profound T-cell
    developmental impairment. CD3G deficiency more often retains peripheral T cells, but severe clinical
    SCID-like CD3G presentations also occur; gene and allele-specific evaluation is required.
  disease_term:
    preferred_term: severe combined immunodeficiency
    term:
      id: MONDO:0015974
      label: severe combined immunodeficiency
  distinguishing_features:
  - Typical profound T-cell depletion in other CD3-chain defects
  - Variable retained T-cell numbers and autoimmunity in CD3G deficiency
  - Gene-specific molecular diagnosis rather than clinical severity alone
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:40147628
    reference_title: Five Patients With Two Novel Homozygous Variants in CD3 Subunits and
      Comprehensive Review of the Literature.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: |-
      Our results show that patients with CD3δ, CD3ε, and CD3ζ deficiencies typically present with a classic SCID phenotype. In contrast, patients with CD3γ deficiency may either show an SCID phenotype or a milder, less severe CID phenotype.
    explanation: >-
      The comparative CD3-subunit analysis supports a typical rather than absolute distinction; its mixed
      49-patient denominator is not assigned to CD3G alone.
- name: Common variable immunodeficiency
  description: >-
    Adult patients with CD3G deficiency have been managed as CVID because of antibody deficiency and low
    memory B cells. Reduced surface TCR/CD3 and biallelic CD3G variants identify the cellular lesion;
    a normal PHA response, as in the Taiwanese patient, does not exclude it.
  disease_term:
    preferred_term: common variable immunodeficiency
    term:
      id: MONDO:0015517
      label: common variable immunodeficiency
  distinguishing_features:
  - Reduced surface TCRalphabeta and CD3 expression
  - Variable proliferative defects; normal PHA does not exclude CD3G deficiency
  - Biallelic CD3G variants on sequencing
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:31921117
    reference_title: "A Novel CD3G Mutation in a Taiwanese Patient With Normal T Regulatory Function Presenting
      With the CVID Phenotype Free of Autoimmunity-Analysis of all Genotypes and Phenotypes."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      This patient had the common variable immunodeficiency (CVID) phenotype and received regular immunoglobulin
      infusions over 20-years; he gradually developed nodular regenerative hyperplasia over a 5-year period.
    explanation: >-
      A CD3gamma-deficient adult managed for two decades as common variable
      immunodeficiency.
animal_models:
- name: Cd3g-deficient mouse
  species: Mouse
  genotype: Cd3g null (selective loss of CD3gamma expression)
  publication: PMID:9524111
  description: >-
    Cd3g-null mice have thymic cellularity below 1% and peripheral T-cell numbers at 2–5% of normal, with
    severe impairment of both TCR lineages. This differs from the substantial peripheral T-cell compartment
    retained by many human patients, although human clinical disease can also be severe. Residual mouse
    cells are not described as wholly absent.
  modeled_mechanisms:
  - target: Impaired TCR/CD3 Complex Assembly and Surface Expression
    relationship: PARTIALLY_RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    description: >-
      The mouse does reproduce the receptor-expression defect: surface TCR and
      CD3epsilon are severely reduced on thymocytes and peripheral T cells.
    limitations: >-
      The developmental and quantitative receptor deficits are more severe than in typical human cases.
      This limits direct translation to mature patient T cells, without proving that no mature-cell question
      can be studied in the model.
    readouts:
    - name: Surface TCR and CD3epsilon expression on thymocytes and peripheral T cells
      target: Impaired TCR/CD3 Complex Assembly and Surface Expression
      direction: DECREASED
      interpretation: >-
        The receptor-expression component of the human lesion is present in the
        mouse.
      evidence:
      - reference: PMID:9524111
        reference_title: The CD3gamma chain is essential for development of both the TCRalphabeta
          and TCRgammadelta lineages.
        supports: SUPPORT
        evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
        snippet: >-
          Furthermore, absence of CD3gamma results in a severe reduction in the level of TCR and CD3epsilon
          expression at the cell surface of thymocytes and peripheral T cells.
        explanation: >-
          Direct measurement of the surface receptor deficit in the model.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:9524111
      reference_title: The CD3gamma chain is essential for development of both the TCRalphabeta and
        TCRgammadelta lineages.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: >-
        We generated mice selectively lacking expression of CD3gamma, in which expression of CD3delta,
        CD3epsilon, CD3zeta, pTalpha and TCRbeta remained undisturbed.
      explanation: >-
        Establishes that the model isolates loss of CD3gamma, the same lesion as
        in patients.
  - target: Distorted Thymic Selection and Self-Reactive Repertoire
    relationship: FAILS_TO_RECAPITULATE
    fidelity: LOW
    description: >-
      The profound mouse developmental bottleneck does not reproduce the preserved, variably skewed peripheral
      repertoire studied in human autoimmune cohorts. The cited experiments do not directly establish
      fidelity for human Treg dysfunction or clinical autoimmunity.
    limitations: >-
      Peripheral mouse T cells are reduced to 2–5%, not absent. Human CD3delta rescues gamma-delta development
      in double-deficient mice, explaining a lineage-specific compensation mechanism; this does not fully
      explain preserved human alpha-beta development or establish all autoimmune outcomes in the model.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:9524111
      reference_title: The CD3gamma chain is essential for development of both the TCRalphabeta and
        TCRgammadelta lineages.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: >-
        The number of cells in the thymus is reduced to <1% of that in normal mice, and the large majority
        of thymocytes lack CD4 and CD8 and are arrested at the CD44-CD25+ double negative (DN) stage of
        development.
      explanation: >-
        The mouse developmental bottleneck is much more profound than that in human cohorts retaining
        peripheral T cells.
    - reference: PMID:9524111
      reference_title: The CD3gamma chain is essential for development of both the TCRalphabeta and
        TCRgammadelta lineages.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: |-
        Peripheral lymphoid organs are also practically devoid of T cells, with absolute numbers of peripheral T cells reduced to only 2-5% of those in normal mice.
      explanation: >-
        The model retains a small peripheral population; the result must not be described as complete
        absence.
    - reference: PMID:17923503
      reference_title: Different composition of the human and the mouse gammadelta T cell receptor
        explains different phenotypes of CD3gamma and CD3delta immunodeficiencies.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: |-
        A human, but not a mouse, CD3delta transgene rescues gammadelta T cell development in mice lacking both mouse CD3delta and CD3gamma chains.
      explanation: >-
        The perturbation demonstrates lineage-specific structural compensation in a mouse model; it does
        not by itself resolve human alpha-beta/Treg biology.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:9524111
    reference_title: The CD3gamma chain is essential for development of both the TCRalphabeta and
      TCRgammadelta lineages.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: |-
      Peripheral lymphoid organs are also practically devoid of T cells, with absolute numbers of peripheral T cells reduced to only 2-5% of those in normal mice.
    explanation: >-
      The model retains a small peripheral population; the result must not be described as complete absence.
